把它唤作科学，丝毫不减它的可憎。」他迟疑着接下去，「不过，您所谓帮衬，我怕有难处。您想，我做美以美会牧师、他做天主教神父这个事实本身，就叫我无从插手。」
「不；那顶不了一个纽扣，」塞莉娅轻巧地说。「我们谁也不往这上头想。」
「那么还有一重难处，」塞隆怯怯地往下说，「福布斯神父在这些学问上，是比我广阔得多、精深得多的学者。我凭我这套可怜的论证，怎么指望影响他？他思考用的那种语言，我连字母都不识——我是说，在这些题目上。」
「您当然不识！」姑娘插进来，那副笃定，纵然塞隆再谦逊，也听得微微一缩。「我根本不是那个意思。我们要朋友的论证做什么：我们要的是同情、是感应、是情感的联结。合宜的人一声不响，做伴也胜过天下顶聪明的人顶聪明的话。这里用得上的不是您的头脑，不是您的所知；是您的心，是您的所感。您一身是诗，是理想，是慷慨的、无私的冲动。您看见事物人性的、热血的一面。*那*才是真正宝贵的东西。*那*才是您帮得上忙的地方！」
「您把我估得太高了，」塞隆抗议，话音里倒对她这份错爱颇为受用。「可您总该跟我讲讲这位莱兹马医生。他说自己是神——是福布斯神父的老朋友。」
「哦，是，他们一直是旧识；就是说，许多年了。他们从前同在一所学院做教授，天知道多少年前了。后来分了手——分手前，我料想还吵过一场。医生到了这儿，住着一处房产，是哪位亲戚留给他的。后来主教碰巧把福布斯神父派到这儿——那是三年前光景——过一阵，两人又续上了旧交。他们一起吃饭；那是医生的堡垒。论吃的学问，我相信活着的人里没有谁赶得上他。他研究吃，像您研究一门语言。他把牧馆的老玛吉，调教得赛过德尔莫尼科全家的老祖宗。

===Original===

And while they sit and stuff themselves, or loll about afterward like gorged snakes, they think it is smart to laugh at all the sweet and beautiful things in life, and to sneer at people who believe in ideals, and to talk about mankind being merely a fortuitous product of fermentation, and twaddle of that sort. It makes me sick!”
“I can readily see,” said Theron, with sympathy, “how such a cold, material, and infidel influence as that must shock and revolt an essentially religious temperament like yours.”
Miss Madden looked up at him. They had turned into the main street, and there was light enough for him to detect something startlingly like a grin on her beautiful face.
“But I’m not religious at all, you know,” he heard her say. “I’m as Pagan as﻿—anything! Of course there are forms to be observed, and so on; I rather like them than otherwise. I can make them serve very well for my own system; for I am myself, you know, an out-an-out Greek.”
“Why, I had supposed that you were full blooded Irish,” the Rev. Mr. Ware found himself remarking, and then on the instant was overwhelmed by the consciousness that he had said a foolish thing. Precisely where the folly lay he did not know, but it was impossible to mistake the gesture of annoyance which his companion had instinctively made at his words. She had widened the distance between them now, and quickened her step. They went on in silence till they were within a block of her house. Several people had passed them who Theron felt sure must have recognized them both.
“What I meant was,” the girl all at once began, drawing nearer again, and speaking with patient slowness, “that I find myself much more in sympathy with the Greek thought, the Greek theology of the beautiful and the strong, the Greek philosophy of life, and all that, than what is taught nowadays. Personally, I take much more stock in Plato than I do in Peter. But of course it is a wholly personal affair; I had no business to bother you with it. And for that matter, I oughtn’t to have troubled you with any of our﻿—”
“I assure you, Miss Madden!” the young minister began, with fervor.
“No,” she broke in, in a resigned and even downcast tone; “let it all be as if I hadn’t spoken. Don’t mind anything I have said. If it is to be, it will be. You can’t say more than that, can you?”

===Chinese===

他们坐在那儿填个满肚子，饭后又像蟒蛇撑饱了似的歪着，还自以为聪明，嘲笑人生一切甜美美好的事物，讥诮信理想的人，大谈人类不过是发酵的偶然产物，诸如此类的废话。叫人恶心！」
「我完全看得出，」塞隆同情地说，「那样一种冷酷、唯物、无神的势力，落在您这样本质上宗教的气质上，是怎样地震骇和作呕。」
马登小姐抬眼望他。两人已拐进正街；灯光够亮，亮得他看清她那张美丽的脸上，有个东西惊人地像龇牙一笑。
「可我压根儿不宗教呀，您知道的，」他听她说。「我异教得很——跟什么都异教！当然，还有几分形式要守，等等；那些我不嫌，反倒喜欢。我可以把它们改造成很适合我自己的一套体系；因为我自己，您知道，是个彻头彻尾的希腊人。」
「哎呀，我还当您是纯血统的爱尔兰人呢，」韦尔牧师发现自己说出了这么一句，随即立刻被自己这句蠢话压倒。蠢在哪里，他并不知道；可同伴闻言本能地一甩那副恼怒的姿态，错认不了的。她此刻拉开了两人间的距离，加快了脚步。两人默默走到离她家不出一个街区的地方。迎面过去了几拨人，塞隆确信他们必定认出了他们两个。
「我的意思是，」姑娘忽然重新开口，又凑近了些，说得耐心而缓慢，「比起如今教的这些，我发现自己同希腊的思想、希腊关于美与力的神学、希腊的人生哲学等等这一套，同情多得多。就我个人说，我对柏拉图的信心，比对彼得的还大。不过这纯粹是私事；我不该拿它搅扰您。为这一点，我也不该拿我们的——」
「我向您保证，马登小姐！」年轻的牧师热烈地开口。
「不，」她打断，声调听天由命，甚至低沉；「就当我什么也没说过。别把我说的任何话放在心上。该来的，总会来。您说不出比这更多的了，对不对？」

===Original===

She looked into his face again, and her large eyes produced an impression of deep melancholy, which Theron found himself somehow impelled to share. Things seemed all at once to have become very sad indeed.
“It is one of my unhappy nights,” she explained, in gloomy confidence. “I get them every once in a while﻿—as if some vicious planet or other was crossing in front of my good star﻿—and then I’m a caution to snakes. I shut myself up﻿—that’s the only thing to do﻿—and have it out with myself I didn’t know but the organ-music would calm me down, but it hasn’t. I shan’t sleep a wink tonight, but just rage around from one room to another, piling all the cushions from the divans on to the floor, and then kicking them away again. Do *you* ever have fits like that?”
Theron was able to reply with a good conscience in the negative. It occurred to him to add, with jocose intent: “I am curious to know, do these fits, as you call them, occupy a prominent part in Grecian philosophy as a general rule?”
Celia gave a little snort, which might have signified amusement, but did not speak until they were upon her own sidewalk. “There is my brother, waiting at the gate,” she said then, briefly.
“Well, then, I will bid you good night here, I think,” Theron remarked, coming to a halt, and offering his hand. “It must be getting very late, and my﻿—that is﻿—I have to be up particularly early tomorrow. So good night; I hope you will be feeling ever so much better in spirits in the morning.”
“Oh, that doesn’t matter,” replied the girl, listlessly. “It’s a very paltry little affair, this life of ours, at the best of it. Luckily it’s soon done with﻿—like a bad dream.”
“Tut! Tut! I won’t have you talk like that!” interrupted Theron, with a swift and smart assumption of authority. “Such talk isn’t sensible, and it isn’t good. I have no patience with it!”
“Well, try and have a little patience with *me*, anyway, just for tonight,” said Celia, taking the reproof with gentlest humility, rather to her censor’s surprise. “I really am unhappy tonight, Mr. Ware, very unhappy. It seems as if all at once the world had swelled out in size a thousandfold, and that poor me had dwindled down to the merest wee little redheaded atom﻿—the most helpless and forlorn and lonesome of atoms at that.”

===Chinese===

她又望进他的脸；那双大眼睛造成一种深深的忧郁的印象，塞隆发现自己竟不由自主要同它共鸣。一切仿佛陡然变得非常悲哀了。
「这是我的一夜愁，」她阴郁地剖白道。「隔一阵子就有一回——仿佛哪颗凶星转到了我吉星的前头——那时节我连蛇都要退避三舍。我把自己关起来——只有这一条路——同自己算总账。我原想管风琴的音乐能把我压下去，可没有。今夜我要一夜合不上眼，只能一间屋子一间屋子地乱转，把躺椅上的软垫全抱到地板上，再一脚一脚踢开。*您*从来没有这样的发作么？」
塞隆能够问心无愧地答一声没有。他还想趁兴添一句打趣：「我倒好奇——您所谓发作，在希腊哲学里照例占显要的一席么？」
塞莉娅轻轻哼了一声，算不得笑，也没答话，直到两人踏上她家门前的人行道。「我哥哥在门口等着，」她简短地说。
「那么，我就在这儿道晚安了，」塞隆说着站住，伸出手。「一定很晚了；我——我是说——我明天要起个特别早。就这样吧，晚安；愿您明早的心绪好上十倍。」
「哦，那不打紧，」姑娘懒懒地答。「我们这条命，往好里说也是顶寒碜的一件小事。幸亏它快就完——像一场噩梦。」
「啧！啧！不许您这样说话！」塞隆打断她，敏捷而漂亮地端起权威。「这样的话不明智，也没有益。我对它没有耐性！」
「那么，就今晚一回，请您对我耐一耐性，」塞莉娅说，把这份训斥以顶温柔的谦卑受了下去——倒叫训人的那位吃了一惊。「我今夜真不快活，韦尔先生，非常不快活。仿佛世界一下子胀大了一千倍，而可怜的我缩成了顶小顶小的一粒红头发原子——还是原子里顶孤苦、顶无靠、顶寂寞的那个。」

===Original===

She seemed to force a sorrowful smile on her face as she added: “But all the same it has done me good to be with you﻿—I am sure it has﻿—and I daresay that by tomorrow I shall be quite out of the blues. Good night, Mr. Ware. Forgive my making such an exhibition of myself. I *was* going to be such a fine early Greek, you know, and I have turned out only a late Milesian﻿—quite of the decadence. I shall do better next time. And good night again, and ever so many thanks.”
She was walking briskly away toward the gate now, where the shadowy Michael still patiently stood. Theron strode off in the opposite direction, taking long, deliberate steps, and bowing his head in thought. He had his hands behind his back, as was his wont, and the sense of their recent contact with her firm, ungloved hands was, curiously enough, the thing which pushed itself uppermost in his mind. There had been a frank, almost manly vigor in her grasp; he said to himself that of course that came from her playing so much on the keyboard; the exercise naturally would give her large, robust hands.
Suddenly he remembered about the piano; he had quite forgotten to solicit her aid in selecting it. He turned, upon the impulse, to go back. She had not entered the gate as yet, but stood, shiningly visible under the street lamp, on the sidewalk, and she was looking in his direction. He turned again like a shot, and started homeward.
The front door of the parsonage was unlocked, and he made his way on tiptoe through the unlighted hall to the living-room. The stuffy air here was almost suffocating with the evil smell of a kerosene lamp turned down too low. Alice sat asleep in her old farmhouse rocking-chair, with an inelegant darning-basket on the table by her side. The whole effect of the room was as bare and squalid to Theron’s newly informed eye as the atmosphere was offensive to his nostrils. He coughed sharply, and his wife sat up and looked at the clock. It was after eleven.
“Where on earth have you been?” she asked, with a yawn, turning up the wick of her sewing-lamp again.
“You ought never to turn down a light like that,” said Theron, with a complaining note in his voice. “It smells up the whole place. I never dreamed of your sitting up for me like this. You ought to have gone to bed.”
“But how could I guess that you were going to be so late?” she retorted.

===Chinese===

她像是强撑出一副悲戚的笑，添道：「可同您在一起到底于我有益——我确知有——到明天，我多半就走出这阵忧郁了。晚安，韦尔先生。原谅我这样当众出丑。您知道，我*本来*要做个多像样的早期希腊人，结果只落得一个晚期的米利西安人——十足颓废派。下回我一定争气。再道一声晚安，多多感谢。」
此刻她已快步朝院门走去，朦胧的迈克尔还耐心地立在那里。塞隆大步朝相反的方向去了，步子又长又稳，低头沉思。他两手照惯例背在身后；说来奇怪，方才同她那双紧实的不戴手套的手相触的感觉，此刻最是涌上心头。她那一握里有种坦率、几乎近于男子的劲道；他对自己说，这自然是弹琴弹出来的——键盘上的功夫，自然会把一双手练得又大又有力。
他猛然想起钢琴的事；竟忘了求她帮着挑琴。他一时冲动，转身要回去。她还没有进门，正立在人行道上，街灯照得她亮亮的，脸朝着他这个方向。他立刻一个急转，撒步回家。
牧师住宅的前门没有上闩；他踮着脚摸黑穿过门厅进了起居室。屋里闷浊的空气，饱浸着一盏煤油灯捻得太低的恶臭，几乎叫人窒息。艾丽斯坐在她那把农家旧摇椅里睡着了，身边桌上搁着一只不入眼的补衣篮。在新近开了眼界的塞隆看来，这屋子整个的气象，同气味之呛鼻一样，又寒酸又逼仄。他重重咳了一声；妻子坐直身子，看了看钟。已过十一点了。
「你到底上哪儿去了？」她打着呵欠问，又把针线灯的捻子挑亮。
「这样压灯，万万使不得，」塞隆说，话音里带着抱怨。「满屋子都是味儿。我做梦也想不到你会这样坐着等我。你该早些睡。」
「可我哪里猜得到你会回来这么晚？」她回嘴。

===Original===

“And you haven’t told me where you were. Is this book of yours going to keep you up like this right along?”
The episode of the book was buried in the young minister’s mind beneath such a mass of subsequent experiences that it required an effort for him to grasp what she was talking about. It seemed as if months had elapsed since he was in earnest about that book; and yet he had left the house full of it only a few hours before. He shook his wits together, and made answer﻿—
“Oh, bless you, no! Only there arose a very curious question. You have no idea, literally no conception, of the interesting and important problems which are raised by the mere fact of Abraham leaving the city of Ur. It’s amazing, I assure you. I hadn’t realized it myself.”
“Well,” remarked Alice, rising﻿—and with good-humor and petulance struggling sleepily ill her tone﻿—“all I’ve got to say is, that if Abraham hasn’t anything better to do than to keep young ministers of the gospel out, goodness knows where, till all hours of the night, I wish to gracious he’d stayed in the city of Ur right straight along.”
“You have no idea what a scholarly man Dr. Ledsmar is,” Theron suddenly found himself inspired to volunteer. “He has the most marvellous collection of books﻿—a whole library devoted to this very subject﻿—and he has put them all quite freely at my disposal. Extremely kind of him, isn’t it?”
“Ledsmar? Ledsmar?” queried Alice. “I don’t seem to remember the name. He isn’t the little man with the birthmark, who sits in the pew behind the Lovejoys, is he? I think someone said *he* was a doctor.”
“Yes, a horse doctor!” said Theron, with a sniff. “No; you haven’t seen this Dr. Ledsmar at all. I﻿—I don’t know that he attends any church regularly. I scraped his acquaintance quite by accident. He is really a character. He lives in the big house, just beyond the racecourse, you know﻿—the one with the tower at the back﻿—”
“No, I *don’t* know. How should I? I’ve hardly poked my nose outside of the yard since I have been here.”
“Well, you shall go,” said the husband, consolingly. “You *have* been cooped up here too much, poor girl. I must take you out more, really. I don’t know that I could take you to the doctor’s place﻿—without an invitation, I mean. He is very queer about some things. He lives there all alone, for instance, with only a Chinaman for a servant.

===Chinese===

「再说你还没告诉我你上哪儿去了。你那本书，往后要夜夜这样熬你么？」
书那一节，在年轻牧师心里早叫随后涌来的一大堆经历压到了底，要费点劲才想得起她说的是什么。仿佛他认真弄那本书已是几个月前的事了；可就在几个钟头前，他还揣着满脑子的它出的门呢。他振作了一下，答道——
「哦，哪能呢！只是半路起了一个非常古怪的问题。亚伯拉罕单单离开吾珥城这一件事，就引出多少有趣而重大的难题——您毫无概念，实实在在毫无概念。我向您担保，惊人得很。连我自己先前也没意识到。」
「哼，」艾丽斯起身说——话音里睡意同嗔怪睡眼惺忪地交战——「我只说一句：倘若亚伯拉罕没有更好的事做，只会把年轻的福音牧师留在不知什么鬼地方直到深更半夜，那我看在老天爷份上，他倒不如一直待在吾珥城里别出来。」
「您不知道莱兹马医生是怎样一位学者，」塞隆忽然发现自己来了兴致，主动卖弄。「他藏书之富叫人惊叹——整整一座图书馆全是这一门的——而且全部慨然供我取用。他实在客气，是不是？」
「莱兹马？莱兹马？」艾丽斯问。「这名字我好像不记得。是不是洛夫乔伊家包厢后头那个带胎记的小个子？我记得有人说*他*是个医生。」
「是，马医！」塞隆嗤了一声。「不；这位莱兹马医生您根本没见过。他——我看他不常上任何教堂。我认识他纯属偶然。他真是个奇人。他住在大宅子里，跑马场过去一点儿——后头带一座塔的那座——」
「不，我*不*知道。我怎么会知道？到这儿以来，我连院门都没怎么出过。」
「回头带您去，」丈夫安慰说。「您确实关在这儿太久了，可怜的姑娘。我真该多带您出去走走。医生那处宅子——我是说，没有邀请怕带您去不得。他有些地方古怪得很。比如，他独自住在那儿，只雇一个中国仆人。

===Original===

He told me I was almost the only man he had asked under his roof for years. He isn’t a practising physician at all, you know. He is a scientist; he makes experiments with lizards﻿—and things.”
“Theron,” the wife said, pausing lamp in hand on her way to the bedroom, “do you be careful, now! For all you know this doctor may be a loose man, or pretty near an infidel. You’ve got to be mighty particular in such matters, you know, or you’ll have the trustees down on you like a thousand of bricks.”
“I will thank the trustees to mind their own business,” said Theron, stiffly, and the subject dropped.
The bedroom window upstairs was open, and upon the fresh night air was borne in the shrill, jangling sound of a piano, being played off somewhere in the distance, but so vehemently that the noise imposed itself upon the silence far and wide. Theron listened to this as he undressed. It proceeded from the direction of the main street, and he knew, as by instinct, that it was the Madden girl who was playing. The incongruity of the hour escaped his notice. He mused instead upon the wild and tropical tangle of moods, emotions, passions, which had grown up in that strange temperament. He found something very pathetic in that picture she had drawn of herself in forecast, roaming disconsolate through her rooms the livelong night, unable to sleep. The woeful moan of insomnia seemed to make itself heard in every strain from her piano.
Alice heard it also, but being unillumined, she missed the romantic pathos. “I call it disgraceful,” she muttered from her pillow, “for folks to be banging away on a piano at this time of night. There ought to be a law to prevent it.”
“It may be some distressed soul,” said Theron, gently, “seeking relief from the curse of sleeplessness.”
The wife laughed, almost contemptuously. “Distressed fiddlesticks!” was her only other comment.
The music went on for a long time﻿—rising now to strident heights, now sinking off to the merest tinkling murmur, and broken ever and again by intervals of utter hush. It did not prevent Alice from at once falling sound asleep; but Theron lay awake, it seemed to him, for hours, listening tranquilly, and letting his mind wander at will through the pleasant antechambers of Sleep, where are more unreal fantasies than Dreamland itself affords.

===Chinese===

他告诉我，多年来他请进家门的男人，我几乎是独一个。他根本不行医，您知道。他是科学家；拿蜥蜴——之类的东西——做实验。」
「塞隆，」妻子提着灯，在去卧房的路上停住，「你可要当心！谁知这医生是不是个行止不端的人，要不就差不离是个不信神的。这种事上你可得十二分检点，不然理事们要劈头盖脸压下来的。」
「理事们管好他们自己的事就够了，多谢，」塞隆生硬地说；这个话题就捾下了。
楼上卧房的窗开着，清新的夜气里飘进来一阵尖利、杂乱的琴声：远处什么地方有人在弹钢琴，弹得那样狂暴，那声响硬是把自己的意志强加给四方的寂静。塞隆一边脱衣一边听。声音来自正街方向；他凭本能就知道，弹琴的是那位马登姑娘。时辰的不合宜，他没有留意。他倒玩味着在那副奇异的气质里长成的这一片狂野而湿热如丛林的种种心绪、情感、激情。她自画的那幅预兆——整夜在自己房间里愁苦地徘徊、无眠——里有种东西，非常凄婉。失眠那悲哀的呻吟，仿佛从她钢琴的每一节乐句里透出来。
艾丽斯也听见了；可她没有这点灵光，错过其中的浪漫凄婉。「不要脸，」她在枕上咕哝，「这么个时辰还叮叮咚咚砸钢琴。就该立个法禁它。」
「兴许是某个受苦的灵魂，」塞隆温和地说，「在向不眠的诅咒讨一点解脱呢。」
妻子笑了，几乎是轻蔑。「受苦的灵魂，见鬼去吧！」这是她仅有的一句评语。
琴声继续了很久——时而拔到刺耳的高处，时而沉成极轻的叮咚絮语，又时时被全然的静默截断。它一点也没妨碍艾丽斯当场酣然入睡；塞隆却醒着——他觉着自己醒了几个钟头——安静地听，任凭心思随意游过睡眠前厅里那些可爱的房间；那里的幻影，比梦境本身所有的还要不真实。

### XI / 十一
===Original===

For some weeks the Rev. Theron Ware saw nothing of either the priest or the doctor, or the interesting Miss Madden.
There were, indeed, more urgent matters to think about. June had come; and every succeeding day brought closer to hand the ordeal of his first Quarterly Conference in Octavius. The waters grew distinctly rougher as his pastoral bark neared this difficult passage.
He would have approached the great event with an easier mind if he could have made out just how he stood with his congregation. Unfortunately nothing in his previous experiences helped him in the least to measure or guess at the feelings of these curious Octavians. Their Methodism seemed to be sound enough, and to stick quite to the letter of the Discipline, so long as it was expressed in formulae. It was its spirit which he felt to be complicated by all sorts of conditions wholly novel to him.
The existence of a line of streetcars in the town, for example, would not impress the casual thinker as likely to prove a rock in the path of peaceful religion. Theron, in his simplicity, had even thought, when he first saw these bobtailed cars bumping along the rails in the middle of the main street, that they must be a great convenience to people living in the outskirts, who wished to get in to church of a Sunday morning. He was imprudent enough to mention this in conversation with one of his new parishioners. Then he learned, to his considerable chagrin, that when this line was built, some years before, a bitter war of words had been fought upon the question of its being worked on the Sabbath day. The then occupant of the Methodist pulpit had so distinguished himself above the rest by the solemnity and fervor of his protests against this insolent desecration of God’s day that the Methodists of Octavius still felt themselves peculiarly bound to hold this horsecar line, its management, and everything connected with it, in unbending aversion. At least once a year they were accustomed to expect a sermon denouncing it and all its impious Sunday patrons. Theron made a mental resolve that this year they should be disappointed.
Another burning problem, which he had not been called upon before to confront, he found now entangled with the mysterious line which divided a circus from a menagerie.

===Chinese===

接连几个星期，塞隆·韦尔牧师既没有见到神父，也没有见到医生，还有那位有趣的马登小姐。
也确实有更紧迫的事要想。六月来了；此后的每一天，都把他第一次奥克塔维乌斯季度议会的考验拉得更近。他这艘牧舟驶近这段险滩，水色分明地更湍急了。
倘若能摸清自己在会众中的立足点，他本可以更安心地走近这件大事。不幸，他以往的经历没有一样能帮他掂量或猜度这些古怪的奥克塔维乌斯人的感情。他们的美以美主义似乎够纯正，凡表现为条文的，都严守教规的字句；可他感到，它的精神被种种他全然陌生的条件搅得错综复杂。
譬如说，城里有条街车线，随随便便的思想者多半想不到它会成为和平宗教路上的礁石。塞隆天真得可以，初见这些拖尾巴的小车沿大街当中的铁轨颠簸驶过时，还想着它对住在外围、礼拜天早晨想进教堂的人必定是一大便利。他不够谨慎，竟对一位新教民提起这一点。于是他大失所望地得知，几年前修这条线时，围绕安息日行车的问题，曾打过一场唇枪舌剑的恶仗。当年占据美以美会讲坛的那一位，因抗议这骄横的渎神日时的庄严与热烈高出侪辈，奥克塔维乌斯的美以美会众至今自觉特别有义务，对这条马车街车线、它的经营者以及同它有关的一切，抱持不移的憎恶。一年至少一回，他们惯于期待一篇谴责它及其一切不敬虔的礼拜天主顾的讲道。塞隆心里打定主意，今年要叫他们失望。
另一桩他从前不劳面对的火烧眉毛的问题，如今他发现缠结在那条把马戏团同兽栏划分开的神秘界线上。

===Original===

Those itinerant tent-shows had never come his way heretofore, and he knew nothing of that fine balancing proportion between ladies in tights on horseback and cages full of deeply educational animals, which, even as the impartial rain, was designed to embrace alike the just and the unjust. There had arisen inside the Methodist society of Octavius some painful episodes, connected with members who took their children “just to see the animals,” and were convicted of having also watched the Rose-Queen of the Arena, in her unequalled flying leap through eight hoops, with an ardent and unashamed eye. One of these cases still remained on the censorial docket of the church; and Theron understood that he was expected to name a committee of five to examine and try it. This he neglected to do.
He was no longer at all certain that the congregation as a whole liked his sermons. The truth was, no doubt, that he had learned enough to cease regarding the congregation as a whole. He could still rely upon carrying along with him in his discourses from the pulpit a large majority of interested and approving faces. But here, unhappily, was a case where the majority did not rule. The minority, relatively small in numbers, was prodigious in virile force.
More than twenty years had now elapsed since that minor schism in the Methodist Episcopal Church, the result of which was the independent body known as Free Methodists, had relieved the parent flock of its principal disturbing element. The rupture came fittingly at that time when all the “isms” of the argumentative fifties were hurled violently together into the melting-pot of civil war. The great Methodist Church, South, had broken bodily off on the question of State Rights. The smaller and domestic fraction of Free Methodism separated itself upon an issue which may be most readily described as one of civilization. The seceders resented growth in material prosperity; they repudiated the introduction of written sermons and organ-music; they deplored the increasing laxity in meddlesome piety, the introduction of polite manners in the pulpit and classroom, and the development of even a rudimentary desire among the younger people of the church to be like others outside in dress and speech and deportment.

===Chinese===

那些流动帐篷戏，此前从没有闯进过他的生涯；他全然不知骑马紧身衣的女士同满笼深富教育意义的动物之间那种精审的配比——它如同无偏的雨水，设计得让义人与不义者一体均沾。奥克塔维乌斯的美以美会内部起了几桩痛心的案子，都同带着孩子“只是看看动物”的会友有关：他们被查实还看了“竞技场玫瑰女王”一气穿过八个铁环的举世无双的飞腾，而且看得眼热心安。其中一案至今还挂在教会的惩戒簿上；塞隆明白，大家指望他指派一个五人委员会来审查审理。他把这事撂下不管了。
会众整体是否喜欢他的讲道，他再也不敢肯定了。实情多半是：他已经学乖了，不再把会众看作一个整体。登坛宣讲时，他仍然指望把大半数饶有兴味、颜首称许的面孔带在身边。可惜，在这里，多数并不能做主。那个少数，人数相对不多，男子汉的劲头却大得惊人。
美以美会那场小小的分裂——其产物便是称作自由美以美会的独立教派——把母羊群卸下了它主要的捣乱成分，至今已有二十多年。那场决裂来得正合其时：争论纷纭的五十年代的种种“主义”，正被猛烈地一齐抛进内战的熔炉。南方美以美大教会，为州权问题整个儿断裂出去。较小而偏于家务的自由美以美一脉，则在一个大体上可以称为“文明”的问题上分了家。出走的那些人怨恨物质繁荣的增长；他们拒斥书面讲道和管风琴的引进；他们痛心于多管闲事的敬虔日渐懈弛，痛心于讲坛和课堂里礼貌风度的入侵，痛心于教会年轻人连衣着、言谈、举止上想同外面的人一样的最初级的愿望，也在滋长。

===Original===

They did battle as long as they could, inside the fold, to restore it to the severely straight and narrow path of primitive Methodism. When the adverse odds became too strong for them, they quitted the church and set up a Bethel for themselves.
Octavius chanced to be one of the places where they were able to hold their own within the church organization. The Methodism of the town had gone along without any local secession. It still held in full fellowship the radicals who elsewhere had followed their unbridled bent into the strongest emotional vagaries﻿—where excited brethren worked themselves up into epileptic fits, and women whirled themselves about in weird religious ecstasies, like dervishes of the Orient, till they fell headlong in a state of trance. Octavian Methodism was spared extravagances of this sort, it is true, but it paid a price for the immunity. The people whom an open split would have taken away remained to leaven and dominate the whole lump. This small advanced section, with its men of a type all the more aggressive from its narrowness, and women who went about solemnly in plain gray garments, with tight-fitting, unadorned, mouse-colored sunbonnets, had not been able wholly to enforce its views upon the social life of the church members, but of its controlling influence upon their official and public actions there could be no doubt.
The situation had begun to unfold itself to Theron from the outset. He had recognized the episodes of the forbidden Sunday milk and of the flowers in poor Alice’s bonnet as typical of much more that was to come. No week followed without bringing some new fulfilment of this foreboding. Now, at the end of two months, he knew well enough that the hitherto dominant minority was hostile to him and his ministry, and would do whatever it could against him.
Though Theron at once decided to show fight, and did not at all waver in that resolve, his courage was in the main of a despondent sort. Sometimes it would flutter up to the point of confidence, or at least hopefulness, when he met with substantial men of the church who obviously liked him, and whom he found himself mentally ranging on his side, in the struggle which was to come. But more often it was blankly apparent to him that, the moment flags were flying and drums on the roll, these amiable fair-weather friends would probably take to their heels.

===Chinese===

他们在羊圈内部尽其所能地战斗，要把它拉回原始美以美主义那条严苛的窄路。等到对手的分量压得他们撑不住了，他们便退出教会，给自己立了一座伯特利。
奥克塔维乌斯碰巧是这样一个地方：他们竟能留在教会组织里面立住脚跟。本城的美以美主义一路走来，没有发生当地的分裂。那些在别处会随着无缰的性子奔向最强烈情感狂潮的激进派——兴奋的弟兄们把自己折腾到癫痫发作，妇女们像东方的托钵僧一样在奇异的宗教狂迷里打旋，直到一头栽倒、人事不省——在这里仍享完全的团契。不错，奥克塔维乌斯的美以美主义幸免了这类的放诞，但这份免疫是付了代价的：一场公开的分裂本会带走的人们留了下来，把整个面团发酵了、支配了。这一小撮先进分子——其中的男人因狭隘而愈显凶悍，女人穿着素净的灰衣、戴着紧匝无饰的鼠色遮阳帽，庄重地走来走去——虽未能把他们的主张全加在会友的社会生活上，但他们左右会友公务和公开行止的力量，是不容怀疑的。
这局面从一开头便向塞隆次第展开。他早认出，礼拜天牛奶之禁和可怜的艾丽斯帽上的花，是往后许多事的典型。此后没有一个星期不来一桩这凶兆的新应验。如今，两个月过去，他心里透亮：这个迄今当权的少数派敌视他本人和他的圣工，凡能下手反他的，都要下手。
塞隆当即决定迎战，这决心也毫不动摇；可他的勇气，大抵是垂头丧气的一种。有时遇见教会里的殷实人物明显喜欢他、他也暗暗把他们列为自己一边的时候，这勇气也会振翅飞到自信、至少是希望的点上。可更多的时候他清清楚楚地看到：一旦旌旗招展、战鼓催征，这些和气的晴天朋友多半要抱头鼠窜。

===Original===

Still, such as they were, his sole hope lay in their support. He must make the best of them. He set himself doggedly to the task of gathering together all those who were not his enemies into what, when the proper time came, should be known as the pastor’s party. There was plenty of apostolic warrant for this. If there had not been, Theron felt that the mere elementary demands of self-defence would have justified his use of strategy.
The institution of pastoral calling, particularly that inquisitorial form of it laid down in the Discipline, had never attracted Theron. He and Alice had gone about among their previous flocks in quite a haphazard fashion, without thought of system, much less of deliberate purpose. Theron made lists now, and devoted thought and examination to the personal tastes and characteristics of the people to be cultivated. There were some, for example, who would expect him to talk pretty much as the Discipline ordained﻿—that is, to ask if they had family prayer, to inquire after their souls, and generally to minister grace to his hearers﻿—and these in turn subdivided themselves into classes, ranging from those who would wish nothing else to those who needed only a mild spiritual flavor. There were others whom he would please much better by not talking shop at all. Although he could ill afford it, he subscribed now for a daily paper that he might have a perpetually renewed source of good conversational topics for these more worldly calls. He also bought several pounds of candy, pleasing in color, but warranted to be entirely harmless, and he made a large mysterious mark on the inside of his new silk hat to remind him not to go out calling without some of this in his pocket for the children.
Alice, he felt, was not helping him in this matter as effectively as he could have wished. Her attitude toward the church in Octavius might best be described by the word “sulky.” Great allowance was to be made, he realized, for her humiliation over the flowers in her bonnet. That might justify her, fairly enough, in being kept away from meeting now and again by headaches, or undefined megrims. But it ought not to prevent her from going about and making friends among the kindlier parishioners who would welcome such a thing, and whom he from time to time indicated to her.

===Chinese===

然而，就这么些人，他唯一的指望全在他们的支持上。他必须把他们用到十足。他闷头干起一桩差事：把一切不是他敌人的人聚拢来，待时机成熟，组成为人所知的“牧师党”。这件事有的是使徒的先例可援。纵使没有，塞隆觉得，单凭自卫的起码要求，也尽够为他的用谋略作辩解了。
牧师拜客这一制度，尤其是教规里规定的那种盘问式的拜客，从来不合塞隆的胃口。从前他和艾丽斯在各自的羊群中走动，全凭兴至，既无系统，更无预定的目的。如今塞隆开起名单，对要结交的人的口味和脾性，花心思一一考查。譬如，有些人指望他大体照教规训示的谈——就是问家里做不做家庭礼拜，问候他们的灵魂，总之向听者输送恩典——这些人又自分等级，从巴不得只谈这个的，到只需一点淡淡的灵性调味的。另一些人，他完全不谈本行倒更能讨他们的好。他订了一份日报——虽然负担不起——好为这些较世俗的拜访备一个常新的谈资之源。他又买了几磅糖果，颜色喜人，保准无害，并在他那顶新丝绸大礼帽的里侧画了一个大大的神秘记号，提醒自己出门拜客，口袋里不装一点给孩子的不许走。
艾丽斯呢，他感到，在这件事上帮他的忙不及他的指望。她对奥克塔维乌斯教会的态度，用“怄气”两个字形容最恰当。对帽上花朵那场羞辱，他明白，尽可以多多体谅。那尽够为她间或因头疼或无名的不快而缺席礼拜作辩护。可它不该拦着她出门，在那些乐于此道的较和善的教民中间交朋友——那些人他还不时指点给她。

===Original===

She did go to some extent, it is true, but she produced, in doing so, an effect of performing a duty. He did not find traces anywhere of her having created a brilliant social impression. When they went out together, he was peculiarly conscious of having to do the work unaided.
This was not at all like the Alice of former years, of other charges. Why, she had been, beyond comparison, the most popular young woman in Tyre. What possessed her to mope like this in Octavius?
Theron looked at her attentively nowadays, when she was unaware of his gaze, to try if her face offered any answer to the riddle. It could not be suggested that she was ill. Never in her life had she been looking so well. She had thrown herself, all at once, and with what was to him an unaccountable energy, into the creation and management of a flower-garden. She was out the better part of every day, rain or shine, digging, transplanting, pruning, pottering generally about among her plants and shrubs. This work in the open air had given her an aspect of physical well-being which it was impossible to be mistaken about.
Her husband was glad, of course, that she had found some occupation which at once pleased her and so obviously conduced to health. This was so much a matter of course, in fact, that he said to himself over and over again that he *was* glad. Only﻿—only, sometimes the thought *would* force itself upon his attention that if she did not spend so much of her time in her own garden, she would have more time to devote to winning friends for them in the Garden of the Lord﻿—friends whom they were going to need badly.
The young minister, in taking anxious stock of the chances for and against him, turned over often in his mind the fact that he had already won rank as a pulpit orator. His sermons had attracted almost universal attention at Tyre, and his achievement before the Conference at Tecumseh, if it did fail to receive practical reward, had admittedly distanced all the other preaching there. It was a part of the evil luck pursuing him that here in this perversely enigmatic Octavius his special gift seemed to be of no use whatever. There were times, indeed, when he was tempted to think that bad preaching was what Octavius wanted.

===Chinese===

她确实也去了几分，可是去的时候，一副履行义务的架势。他到处也寻不见她留下过什么亮丽的社交印象。两人一同出门时，他格外意识到，活儿全得他一个人干。
这全然不像从前别的教区的那个艾丽斯了。嗐，她可是泰尔无可比拟的最受欢迎的年轻女子啊。是什么迷了她的心窍，叫她在奥克塔维乌斯这样闷闷不乐？
如今，趁她不察觉，塞隆细细端详她，想从她脸上找出这谜的答案。说她病了，说不上。她这辈子从没有气色这样好过。她忽然一头扎进一座花园的营造和管理里，那股劲头，在他看来无从索解。每天的大半时光她都在外面，风里雨里，掘土、移栽、修剪，在花草灌木间到处拾掇。这份露天的工作给了她一副身体康健的容色，想认错也认不了。
她的丈夫自然高兴，她找到了一样既中她的意、又明摆着有益健康的事做。这原是天经地义，事实上他也一遍又一遍对自己说，他*是*高兴。只是——只是，有时那念头偏要挤进他的注意：倘若她不把这么多光阴花在自己的园子里，她本可以多些工夫，到主的园子里去为他们赢得朋友——他们往后要大大地用得着的朋友。
年轻的牧师焦灼地清点着胜负的筹码，心里反复拣量一个事实：他早已挣得讲坛演说家的名分。他的讲道在泰尔几乎人人注目；他在特库姆塞议会上的那番表现，虽未得到实际的报偿，公认把那里其余的讲道统统甩在了后头。这也是缠着他的坏运气的一部分：在这个执拗成谜的奥克塔维乌斯，他的特殊禀赋似乎毫无用处。有些时候，他简直要疑心，奥克塔维乌斯要的正是坏讲道。

===Original===

Somewhere he had heard of a Presbyterian minister, in charge of a big city church, who managed to keep well in with a watchfully Orthodox congregation, and at the same time establish himself in the affections of the community at large, by simply preaching two kinds of sermons. In the morning, when almost all who attended were his own communicants, he gave them very cautious and edifying doctrinal discourses, treading loyally in the path of the Westminster Confession. To the evening assemblages, made up for the larger part of outsiders, he addressed broadly liberal sermons, literary in form, and full of respectful allusions to modern science and the philosophy of the day. Thus he filled the church at both services, and put money in its treasury and his own fame before the world. There was of course the obvious danger that the pious elders who in the forenoon heard infant damnation vigorously proclaimed, would revolt when they heard after supper that there was some doubt about even adults being damned at all. But either because the same people did not attend both services, or because the minister’s perfect regularity in the morning was each week regarded as a retraction of his latest vagaries of an evening, no trouble ever came.
Theron had somewhat tentatively tried this on in Octavius. It was no good. His parishioners were of the sort who would have come to church eight times a day on Sunday, instead of two, if occasion offered. The hope that even a portion of them would stop away, and that their places would be taken in the evening by less prejudiced strangers who wished for intellectual rather than theological food, fell by the wayside. The yearned-for strangers did not come; the familiar faces of the morning service all turned up in their accustomed places every evening. They were faces which confused and disheartened Theron in the daytime. Under the gaslight they seemed even harder and more unsympathetic. He timorously experimented with them for an evening or two, then abandoned the effort.
Once there had seemed the beginning of a chance. The richest banker in Octavius﻿—a fat, sensual, hog-faced old bachelor﻿—surprised everybody one evening by entering the church and taking a seat.

===Chinese===

他在哪儿听说过一位长老会牧师，主持一座大城市教堂，一面同一个警惕的正统会众相安无事，一面又笼络住全社会的感情，办法无非是讲两种道。早晨，到场的几乎全是自家领圣餐的信徒，他便给他们非常谨慎而有益灵性的教义讲章，忠诚地走在威斯敏斯特信纲的路径上。对晚间的会众——大半是外路人——他讲的则是宽宏开明的道，形式近于文学，满是对现代科学和当日哲学的敬意殷殷的引述。如此两堂皆满座，既给教会金库进了钱，又把自己的名声推到了世人面前。当然，明摆着有一个危险：敬虔的长老们上午刚听完婴儿地狱被大力宣告，晚饭后又听到连成人到底下不下地狱也有些疑问，怕要反了。可是不知是因为两堂听众多半不是同一拨人，还是因为这位牧师上午的始终如一，每星期都被当作对他前一晚最新的邪说的收回，总之祸事从没有来过。
塞隆在奥克塔维乌斯试着照方抓了一回药，不大灵。他的教民属于那种人：礼拜天倘有机会，一天上八回教堂也肯，何况两回。指望哪怕一部分人晚间缺席、让位给那些要精神食粮而非神学食粮的成见较少的生客——这指望半路夭折了。渴望的生客没有来；晨祷的那些熟面孔，每晚又一一张在他们惯常的位子上。这些面孔白天已叫塞隆心乱意冷，到了煤气灯下，显得更硬、更不近人情。他战战兢兢地对他们试验了一两个晚上，便放弃了这番努力。
有一回，似乎起过一线机会的苗头。奥克塔维乌斯最有钱的银行家——一个肥胖、肉感、猪脸的老单身汉——一天晚上进了教堂，拣了个座位，叫所有的人都吃了一惊。

===Original===

Theron happened to know who he was; even if he had not known, the suppressed excitement visible in the congregation, the way the sisters turned round to look, the way the more important brethren put their heads together and exchanged furtive whispers﻿—would have warned him that big game was in view. He recalled afterward with something like self-disgust the eager, almost tremulous pains he himself took to please this banker. There was a part of the sermon, as it had been written out, which might easily give offence to a single man of wealth and free notions of life. With the alertness of a mental gymnast, Theron ran ahead, excised this portion, and had ready when the gap was reached some very pretty general remarks, all the more effective and eloquent, he felt, for having been extemporized. People said it was a good sermon; and after the benediction and dispersion some of the officials and principal pewholders remained to talk over the likelihood of a capture having been effected. Theron did not get away without having this mentioned to him, and he was conscious of sharing deeply the hope of the brethren﻿—with the added reflection that it would be a personal triumph for himself into the bargain. He was ashamed of this feeling a little later, and of his trick with the sermon. But this chastening product of introspection was all the fruit which the incident bore. The banker never came again.
Theron returned one afternoon, a little earlier than usual, from a group of pastoral calls. Alice, who was plucking weeds in a border at the shady side of the house, heard his step, and rose from her labors. He was walking slowly, and seemed weary. He took off his high hat, as he saw her, and wiped his brow. The broiling June sun was still high overhead. Doubtless it was its insufferable heat which was accountable for the worn lines in his face and the spiritless air which the wife’s eye detected. She went to the gate, and kissed him as he entered.
“I believe if I were you,” she said, “I’d carry an umbrella such scorching days as this. Nobody’d think anything of it. I don’t see why a minister shouldn’t carry one as much as a woman carries a parasol.”
Theron gave her a rueful, meditative sort of smile. “I suppose people really do think of us as a kind of hybrid female,” he remarked.

===Chinese===

塞隆恰好知道他是谁；即便不知道，会众里压着的兴奋、姊妹们回头张望的样子、要紧的弟兄们凑头交换眼色的样子，也足以警告他：大猎物在场。他后来想起自己讨好那位银行家时那份急切、几乎发颤的殷勤，几乎要作呕。讲章里照原稿有一节，很容易开罪一个有钱而人生观自由的单身汉。塞隆像个体操家那样心里抢先一步，把这一节剜去，赶到缺口处备好了几句极漂亮的泛泛之言——正因是临场即兴，他自觉愈发有力、愈发雄辩。大家说这是篇好道；祝福散会之后，几位执事和主要座主还留下来议论这头猎物是否已经套住。这话免不了传到塞隆耳朵里；他自觉深深共享着弟兄们的希望——外加一层盘算：这于他个人也是一场胜利，一并算上。过了一阵他为这份感觉、为他在讲道上做的手脚害臊。可内省的这一番惩戒性产品，便是此案结出的全部果实了。那位银行家再没有来过。
一天下午，塞隆拜客回来得比平时略早。艾丽斯正在屋子阴面的一畦花间拔草，听见脚步声，从活计里直起身来。他走得慢，像累了。一见她，他摘下高顶帽，抹了抹额头。灼人的六月的日头还高悬头顶。他脸上那些倦容、他妻子眼里那副无精打采的神气，多半正要归咎于它那难当的酷热。她走到院门口，在他进院时吻了他。
“我要是你，”她说，“这么毒的日头天就带把伞。谁也不会多想的。我不明白，牧师为什么就不能像女人带阳伞一样带把伞。”
塞隆给了她一个懊丧而沉思的笑。“想来人家真把我们当成一种雌雄同体的女人哩。”他说。

===Original===

Then, holding his hat in his hand, he drew a long breath of relief at finding himself in the shade, and looked about him.
“Why, you’ve got more posies here, on this one side of the house alone, than mother had in her whole yard,” he said, after a little. “Let’s see﻿—I know that one: that’s columbine, isn’t it? And that’s London pride, and that’s ragged robin. I don’t know any of the others.”
Alice recited various unfamiliar names, as she pointed out the several plants which bore them, and he listened with a kindly semblance of interest.
They strolled thus to the rear of the house, where thick clumps of fragrant pinks lined both sides of the path. She picked some of these for him, and gave him more names with which to label the considerable number of other plants he saw about him.
“I had no idea we were so well provided as all this,” he commented at last. “Those Van Sizers must have been tremendous hands for flowers. You were lucky in following such people.”
“Van Sizers!” echoed Alice, with contempt. “All they left was old tomato cans and clamshells. Why, I’ve put in every blessed one of these myself, all except those peonies, there, and one brier on the side wall.”
“Good for you!” exclaimed Theron, approvingly. Then it occurred to him to ask, “But where did you get them all? Around among our friends?”
“Some few,” responded Alice, with a note of hesitation in her voice. “Sister Bult gave me the verbenas, there, and the white pinks were a present from Miss Stevens. But most of them Levi Gorringe was good enough to send me﻿—from his garden.”
“I didn’t know that Gorringe had a garden,” said Theron. “I thought he lived over his law-office, in the brick block, there.”
“Well, I don’t know that it’s exactly *his*,” explained Alice; “but it’s a big garden somewhere outside, where he can have anything he likes.” She went on with a little laugh: “I didn’t like to question him too closely, for fear he’d think I was looking a gift horse in the mouth﻿—or else hinting for more. It was quite his own offer, you know. He picked them all out for me, and brought them here, and lent me a book telling me just what to do with each one. And in a few days, now, I am to have another big batch of plants﻿—dahlias and zinnias and asters and so on; I’m almost ashamed to take them. But it’s such a change to find someone in this Octavius who isn’t all self!”

===Chinese===

随后他帽子拿在手里，到了荫凉地，宽慰地长吸一口气，四下望了望。
“嗬，光房子这一面，你的花儿就比母亲整个院子里的还多，”过了一会儿他说。“让我认认——这个我认得：是楼斗菜，是不是？那是虎耳草‘伦敦骄傲’，那是剪秋罗。别的我一样也不认得了。”
艾丽斯一一指着那几样花草，报出种种生疏的名字，他做出和颜悦色的兴味听着。
两人这样信步走到屋后，小径两旁密密排着芬香的石竹。她摘了几朵给他，又给他更多的名字，好去标注他望见的那相当一批花草。
“想不到咱们竟有这么丰足的储备，”他末了说，“范·赛泽家那几位准是了不起的爱花人。跟着这样的人家，你运气好。”
“范·赛泽家！”艾丽斯轻蔑地应了一声，“他们留下的只有番茄罐和蛤蜊壳。嗐，这园子里的每一棵都是我亲手栽的，只有那边那几棵芍药，还有侧墙上一棵野蔷薇除外。”
“好样的！”塞隆赞叹道。随即想起该问一句：“那这些都是从哪儿弄来的？咱们的朋友们中间？”
“有几样，”艾丽斯答道，嗓音里带着一丝迟疑。“巴尔特姊妹给了我那边那些美女樱，白石竹是史蒂文斯小姐送的。可大多数，是利维·戈林奇好心送我的——从他园子里来的。”
“我不知道戈林奇还有园子，”塞隆说，“我还当他住在那排砖楼里他的律师事务所楼上呢。”
“嗯，那园子也说不准*就是*他的，”艾丽斯解释道，“是外头什么地方一座大园子，他爱拿什么就拿什么。”她笑着接下去：“我不敢问得太细，怕他当我是对送来的马看牙口——要不然当我是暗示再要一些。完全是他自己开口的，你知道。都是他替我挑的，运到这儿来，还借给我一本书，每一样该怎么伺候，写得明明白白。再过几天，我还要收大大一批——大丽花、百日菊、翠菊等等；我都有点不好意思收了。在奥克塔维乌斯，难得碰上一个不全是为自己打算的人，这真是个变化！”

===Original===

“Yes, Gorringe is a good fellow,” said Theron. “I wish he was a professing member.” Then some new thought struck him. “Alice,” he exclaimed, “I believe I’ll go and see him this very afternoon. I don’t know why it hasn’t occurred to me before: he’s just the man whose advice I need most. He knows these people here; he can tell me what to do.”
“Aren’t you too tired now?” suggested Alice, as Theron put on his hat.
“No, the sooner the better,” he replied, moving now toward the gate.
“Well,” she began, “if I were you, I wouldn’t say too much about﻿—that is, I﻿—but never mind.”
“What is it?” asked her husband.
“Nothing whatever,” replied Alice, positively. “It was only some nonsense of mine;” and Theron, placidly accepting the feminine whim, went off down the street again.

===Chinese===

“是啊，戈林奇是个好人，”塞隆说，“但愿他是个入会的教友。”随即一个新念头击中了他。“艾丽斯，”他喊道，“我看今天下午我就去看他。不明白怎么先前没有想到：他正是我最需要请教的人。他认识这里的这些人；他能告诉我该怎么办。”
“你这会儿不累么？”塞隆戴帽子时，艾丽斯提示道。
“不，越快越好。”他答着，朝院门走去。
“那么，”她开口道，“我要是你，就不会把……说得太多——就是说，我——算了，没什么。”
“怎么？”她丈夫问。
“没什么，真的，”艾丽斯断然答道，“不过是我的一句傻话。”塞隆安然领受了这女人家的一时兴起，又沿街去了。

### XII / 十二
===Original===

The Rev. Mr. Ware found Levi Gorringe’s law-office readily enough, but its owner was not in. He probably would be back again, though, in a quarter of an hour or so, the boy said, and the minister at once decided to wait.
Theron was interested in finding that this office-boy was no other than Harvey﻿—the lad who brought milk to the parsonage every morning. He remembered now that he had heard good things of this urchin, as to the hard work he did to help his mother, the Widow Semple, in her struggle to keep a roof over her head; and also bad things, in that he did not come regularly either to church or Sunday-school. The clergyman recalled, too, that Harvey had impressed him as a character.
“Well, sonny, are you going to be a lawyer?” he asked, as he seated himself by the window, and looked about him, first at the dusty litter of old papers, pamphlets, and tape-bound documents in bundles which crowded the stuffy chamber, and then at the boy himself.
Harvey was busy at a big box﻿—a rough pine dry-goods box which bore the flaring label of an express company, and also of a well-known seed firm in a Western city, and which the boy had apparently just opened. He was lifting from it, and placing on the table after he had shaken off the sawdust and moss in which they were packed, small parcels of what looked in the fading light to be half-dried plants.
“Well, I don’t know﻿—I rather guess not,” he made answer, as he pursued his task. “So far as I can make out, this wouldn’t be the place to start in at, if I *was* going to be a lawyer. A boy can learn here first-rate how to load cartridges and clean a gun, and braid trout-flies on to leaders, but I don’t see much law laying around loose. Anyway,” he went on, “I couldn’t afford to read law, and not be getting any wages. I have to earn money, you know.”
Theron felt that he liked the boy. “Yes,” he said, with a kindly tone; “I’ve heard that you are a good, industrious youngster. I daresay Mr. Gorringe will see to it that you get a chance to read law, and get wages too.”
“Oh, I can read all there is here and welcome,” the boy explained, stepping toward the window to decipher the label on a bundle of roots in his hand, “but that’s no good unless there’s regular practice coming into the office all the while. *That’s* how you learn to be a lawyer. But Gorringe don’t have what I call a practice at all.

===Chinese===

韦尔牧师没费什么劲就找到了利维·戈林奇的律师事务所，只是主人不在。不过他一刻钟上下多半会回来，那孩子说；牧师当即决定等一等。
塞隆饶有兴味地发现，这个事务所的听差不是别人，正是哈维（Harvey）——每天早上给牧师住宅送牛奶的那个少年。他记起来了：关于这个小家伙，他听说过好话——说他拼命做工，帮母亲森普尔寡妇（Widow Semple）挣一个屋顶遮头；也听说过坏话——说他上教堂、上主日学校都不按时。牧师还记起，哈维当初给他留过印象，是个有性格的孩子。
「喂，小伙子，你想当律师么？」他问，一面在窗边坐下，四下打量：先看这间闷屋子里挤挤挨挨的积尘的烂摊子——旧报纸、小册子、一捆捆绸带扎的文书——再看这孩子本身。
哈维正对着一口大箱子忙着——一口粗糙的松木干货箱，箱皮上贴着一家捷运公司鲜亮的签，还有西部某城一家有名种子行的签，看样子是这孩子刚捾开的。他正从箱里往外捧东西，抖掉包着它们的锯末和苔藓，再摆上桌：一小包一小包的，在渐暗的光线里看着像半干的植株。
「唔，说不好——我看当不成，」他一面干活一面答。「照我琢磨，就算我*真*想当律师，这地方也不是起点。在这儿，一个男孩倒能头一等学装弹壳、擦枪、往钓线上编鳟鱼毛钩，可法律嘛，我看不见有几条撒在地上。再说，」他接着说，「读法律不挣工钱，我供不起。我得挣钱，您知道的。」
塞隆觉得自己喜欢这孩子。「是啊，」他用和善的口吻说；「听说你是个又勤快又正派的小伙子。我想戈林奇先生会关照，叫你既有机会读法律，又有工钱拿。」
「哦，这里有的书我尽管读，欢迎读，」孩子解释着，走到窗前辨认手里一包根须上的标签，「可要是没有正经常务源源进这个门，读了也白读。*那*才是学做律师的路子。戈林奇根本就没有我所谓的事务。

===Original===

He just sees men in the other room there, with the door shut, and whatever there is to do he does it all himself.”
The minister remembered a stray hint somewhere that Mr. Gorringe was a moneylender﻿—what was colloquially called a “note-shaver.” To his rustic sense, there was something not quite nice about that occupation. It would be indecorous, he felt, to encourage further talk about it from the boy.
“What are you doing there?” he inquired, to change the subject.
“Sorting out some plants,” replied Harvey. “I don’t know what’s got into Gorringe lately. This is the third big box he’s had since I’ve been here﻿—that is, in six weeks﻿—besides two baskets full of rosebushes. I don’t know what he does with them. He carries them off himself somewhere. I’ve had kind of half a notion that he’s figurin’ on getting married. I can’t think of anything else that would make a man spend money like water﻿—just for flowers and bushes. They do get foolish, you know, when they’ve got marriage on the brain.”
Theron found himself only imperfectly following the theories of the young philosopher. It was his fact that monopolized the minister’s attention.
“But as I understand it,” he remarked hesitatingly, “Brother Gorringe﻿—or rather Mr. Gorringe﻿—gets all the plants he wants, everything he likes, from a big garden somewhere outside. I don’t know that it is exactly *his*; but I remember hearing something to that effect.”
The boy slapped the last litter off his hands, and, as he came to the window, shook his head. “These don’t come from no garden outside,” he declared. “They come from the dealers’, and he pays solid cash for ’em. The invoice for this lot alone was thirty-one dollars and sixty cents. There it is on the table. You can see it for yourself.”
Mr. Ware did not offer to look. “Very likely these are *for* the garden I was speaking of,” he said. “Of course you can’t go on taking plants out of a garden indefinitely without putting others in.”
“I don’t know anything about any garden that he takes plants *out* of,” answered Harvey, and looked meditatively for a minute or two out upon the street below. Then he turned to the minister. “Your wife’s doing a good deal of gardening this spring, I notice,” he said casually. “You’d hardly think it was the same place, she’s fixed it up so. If she wants any extra hoeing done, I can always get off Saturday afternoons.”

===Chinese===

他只是把人让进那边那间屋，门一关；有什么要办的，全是他自己一手办。」
牧师记起不知在哪里飘来的一句闲话：戈林奇先生是个放债的——俗话所谓「贴票鬼」。照他乡下的心思，这行当有几分不体面。他觉得，再引这孩子往下谈论它，未免失礼。
「你在那儿做什么呢？」他换了话题问。
「给花草分分类，」哈维答。「不知道戈林奇近来中了什么邪。这是我在这里的六个星期里他收的第三口大箱子——还不算满满两篮玫瑰秧。他拿它们干什么，我不知道。总是他自己抱到什么地方去。我倒隐隐疑心，他是盘算着娶亲。除这个，我想不出还有什么能叫一个男人把钱花得像流水——光花在花草灌木上。男人脑子里一装上婚事，你知道，就都犯傻。」
塞隆发觉自己跟不上这位小哲学家的理论了。占住牧师全部心神的，是那个事实。
「可照我的理解，」他迟疑地说，「戈林奇弟兄——不如说戈林奇先生——他想要的一切花草、一切他喜欢的，都从城外哪个大花园里来。我不敢说那园子就*是*他的；可我记得听人这样讲过。」
孩子把手上最后一点碎屑拍掉，走到窗前，摇了摇头。「这些可不是哪个园子里来的，」他断言。「它们是种苗行来的，他付的是响当当的现钱。光这一批，发货单就是三十一美元六十美分。喏，单子在桌上。您自己看。」
韦尔先生没有去看。「这些多半是*替*我说的那个园子买的，」他说。「园子里的花草，光取不补，自然不能无限期取下去。」
「他*从*什么园子里取花草，我可一点不知道，」哈维答，沉思地朝楼下的街望了一两分钟。随后他转向牧师。「您太太今年春天种了不少花草呢，我留意到了，」他随口说。「她拾掇得那样好，简直认不出是原来那地方了。她要是想加点锄草的活，星期六下午我总能抽出身来。」

===Original===

“I will remember,” said Theron. He also looked out of the window; and nothing more was said until, a few moments later, Mr. Gorringe himself came in.
The lawyer seemed both surprised and pleased at discovering the identity of his visitor, with whom he shook hands in almost an excess of cordiality. He spread a large newspaper over the pile of seedling plants on the table, pushed the packing-box under the table with his foot, and said almost peremptorily to the boy, “You can go now!” Then he turned again to Theron.
“Well, Mr. Ware, I’m glad to see you,” he repeated, and drew up a chair by the window. “Things are going all right with you, I hope.”
Theron noted again the waving black hair, the dark skin, and the carefully trimmed mustache and chin-tuft which gave the lawyer’s face a combined effect of romance and smartness. No; it was the eyes, cool, shrewd, dark-gray eyes, which suggested this latter quality. The recollection of having seen one of them wink, in deliberate hostility of sarcasm, when those other trustees had their backs turned, came mercifully at the moment to recall the young minister to his errand.
“I thought I would drop in and have a chat with you,” he said, getting better under way as he went on. “Quarterly Conference is only a fortnight off, and I am a good deal at sea about what is going to happen.”
“I’m not a church member, you know,” interposed Gorringe. “That shuts me out of the Quarterly Conference.”
“Alas, yes!” said Theron. “I wish it didn’t. I’m afraid I’m not going to have any friends to spare there.”
“What are you afraid of?” asked the lawyer, seeming now to be wholly at his ease again. “They can’t eat you.”
“No, they keep me too lean for that,” responded Theron, with a pensive smile. “I *was* going to ask, you know, for an increase of salary, or an extra allowance. I don’t see how I can go on as it is. The sum fixed by the last Quarterly Conference of the old year, and which I am getting now, is one hundred dollars less than my predecessor had. That isn’t fair, and it isn’t right. But so far from its looking as if I could get an increase, the prospect seems rather that they will make me pay for the gas and that sidewalk. I never recovered more than about half of my moving expenses, as you know, and﻿—and, frankly, I don’t know which way to turn. It keeps me miserable all the while.”
“That’s where you’re wrong,” said Mr. Gorringe.

===Chinese===

「我会记住的，」塞隆说。他也望向窗外；随后再没有话，直到片刻后戈林奇先生本人进来。
律师辨明来客是谁，又惊又喜，同他握手，热络得几乎过了头。他把一大张报纸展开，盖住桌上那堆幼苗，用脚把木箱踢进桌底，几乎用命令的口气对孩子说：「现在你可以走了！」随后又转向塞隆。
「好，韦尔先生，见到您真高兴，」他又说了一遍，拉过一把椅子到窗边。「您诸事都还顺当，我希望。」
塞隆又一次注意到那波浪般的黑发、深色的皮肤、修剪齐整的髭须和颏须——它们合起来给律师的脸添上一种既浪漫又精明的总效果。不；是那双眼睛，冷静、锐利、深灰的眼睛，暗示出后一种品质。他适时地记起：那三位理事背过身去的时候，这双眼睛里有一只曾蓄意地、讥诮地眨过一下。这回忆来得正好，把年轻牧师唤回他的来意。
「我想顺便进来，同您聊聊，」他说，越说越顺。「季议会再有两个星期就开了，事情会怎样，我心里很没底。」
「我不是教会会员，您知道的，」戈林奇插话。「这就把我关在季议会门外了。」
「可惜，正是！」塞隆说。「但愿不是这样。只怕到时候我一个多余的朋友也没有。」
「您怕什么？」律师问，此刻仿佛又完全自在了。「他们吃不了您。」
「吃不了，他们把我养得太瘦了，」塞隆答，带着一个沉思的笑。「我*原*打算，您知道的，要求加薪，或一笔额外津贴。照现在这样，我维持不下去。旧年最后一次季议会定的数目——也就是我如今拿的——比我前任少了一百美元。这不公道，也不正当。可如今非但看不出加薪的指望，前景倒是他们要叫我认下煤气账和那条人行道。搬家的费用，您知道的，我统共只捞回大约一半，还有——还有，说实话，我不知道该往哪边转。这事一天到晚折磨着我。」
「您这就错了，」戈林奇先生说。

===Original===

“If you let things like that worry you, you’ll keep a sore skin all your life. You take my advice and just go ahead your own gait, and let other folks do the worrying. They *are* pretty closefisted here, for a fact, but you can manage to rub along somehow. If you should get into any real difficulties, why, I guess﻿—” the lawyer paused to smile in a hesitating, significant way﻿—“I guess some road out can be found all right. The main thing is, don’t fret, and don’t allow your wife to﻿—to fret either.”
He stopped abruptly. Theron nodded in recognition of his amiable tone, and then found the nod lengthening itself out into almost a bow as the thought spread through his mind that this had been nothing more nor less than a promise to help him with money if worst came to worst. He looked at Levi Gorringe, and said to himself that the intuition of women was wonderful. Alice had picked him out as a friend of theirs merely by seeing him pass the house.
“Yes,” he said; “I am specially anxious to keep my wife from worrying. She was surrounded in her girlhood by a good deal of what, relatively, we should call luxury, and that makes it all the harder for her to be a poor minister’s wife. I had quite decided to get her a hired girl, come what might, but she thinks she’d rather get on without one. Her health is better, I must admit, than it was when we came here. She works out in her garden a great deal, and that seems to agree with her.”
“Octavius *is* a healthy place﻿—that’s generally admitted,” replied the lawyer, with indifference. He seemed not to be interested in Mrs. Ware’s health, but looked intently out through the window at the buildings opposite, and drummed with his fingers on the arms of his chair.
Theron made haste to revert to his errand. “Of course, your not being in the Quarterly Conference,” he said, “renders certain things impossible. But I didn’t know but you might have some knowledge of how matters are going, what plans the officials of the church had; they seem to have agreed to tell me nothing.”
“Well, I *have* heard this much,” responded Gorringe. “They’re figuring on getting the Soulsbys here to raise the debt and kind o’ shake things up generally. I guess that’s about as good as settled. Hadn’t you heard of it?”
“Not a breath!” exclaimed Theron, mournfully. “Well,” he added upon reflection, “I’m sorry, downright sorry.

===Chinese===

「这样的事您由它去熬心，一辈子都得熬出疤来。听我的劝：只管照自己的步子走，让旁人去操心。这里的人确实抠，不假；可您总能想法子对付过去。倘若真陷到什么难处里，那个——我想——」律师顿住，笑得迟疑而有深意——「我想，总归找得出一条出路。要紧的是别发愁；也别叫您的太太——发愁。」
他猛地收住。塞隆点头，领受这份好意；随即又觉出这点头越点越长，几乎成了鞠躬——一个念头漫过他的心：这不折不扣是一句许诺——倘若落到最坏，他肯借钱帮他。他望着利维·戈林奇，暗自说：女人的直觉真了不得。艾丽斯只看见他从门前走过，就认定他是他们的朋友。
「是，」他说；「我最怕的就是叫太太操心。她做姑娘时，四下里过的，相对说来，是相当优裕的日子；这使她做一个穷牧师的太太，加倍难熬。我原打定了主意，无论如何要给她雇一个女佣，可她说宁愿不要。她的身子，我必须承认，比我们刚来时好多了。她成天在园子里做活，那似乎对她相宜。」
「奥克塔维乌斯*确实*是个有益健康的地方——大家公认，」律师淡淡地答。他对韦尔太太的健康似乎并无兴致，只顾凝神望着窗外对面的房子，手指在椅子扶手上敲着。
塞隆赶忙回到正题。「当然，您不在季议会里，」他说，「有些事就办不成了。不过我想，您总该知道些风色：教会的那班执事们有些什么打算；他们像是商量好了，什么都不告诉我。」
「唔，这么多我是听说了，」戈林奇应道。「他们正盘算把索尔斯比（Soulsby）两口子请来，募那笔债，顺带把整个局面抖擞抖擞。我看差不多算定局了。您没听说？」
「一点风也没听着！」塞隆悲叹道。「唉，」他想了想又添一句，「我难过，真难过。

===Original===

The debt-raiser seems to me about the lowest-down thing we produce. I’ve heard of those Soulsbys; I think I saw *him* indeed once at Conference, but I believe *she* is the head of the firm.”
“Yes; she wears the breeches, I understand,” said Gorringe sententiously.
“I *had* hoped,” the young minister began with a rueful sigh, “in fact, I felt quite confident at the outset that I could pay off this debt, and put the church generally on a new footing, by giving extra attention to my pulpit work. It is hardly for me to say it, but in other places where I have been, my preaching has been rather﻿—rather a feature in the town itself. I have always been accustomed to attract to our services a good many nonmembers, and that, as you know, helps tremendously from a money point of view. But somehow that has failed here. I doubt if the average congregations are a whit larger now than they were when I came in April. I know the collections are not.”
“No,” commented the lawyer, slowly; “you’ll never do anything in that line in Octavius. You might, of course, if you were to stay here and work hard at it for five or six years﻿—”
“Heaven forbid!” groaned Mr. Ware.
“Quite so,” put in the other. “The point is that the Methodists here are a little set by themselves. I don’t know that they like one another specially, but I do know that they are not what you might call popular with people outside. Now, a new preacher at the Presbyterian church, or even the Baptist﻿—he might have a chance to create talk, and make a stir. But Methodist﻿—no! People who don’t belong won’t come near the Methodist church here so long as there’s any other place with a roof on it to go to. Give a dog a bad name, you know. Well, the Methodists here have got a bad name; and if you could preach like Henry Ward Beecher himself you wouldn’t change it, or get folks to come and hear you.”
“I see what you mean,” Theron responded. “I’m not particularly surprised myself that Octavius doesn’t love us, or look to us for intellectual stimulation. I myself leave that pulpit more often than otherwise feeling like a wet rag﻿—utterly limp and discouraged. But, if you don’t mind my speaking of it, *you* don’t belong, and yet *you* come.”
It was evident that the lawyer did not mind. He spoke freely in reply. “Oh, yes, I’ve got into the habit of it.

===Chinese===

募债这号人，在我看来是我们这行当出产的最下等的东西。索尔斯比这两口子我听说过；我想我还在议会上见过*他*一回；可我相信，*她*才是那家字号的首脑。」
「是；穿裤子的是她，我听说，」戈林奇一板一眼地说。
「我*原本*指望，」年轻的牧师一声苦叹开了头，「说实在的，起初我相当有把握：只要我在讲坛上加倍用心，就能偿清这笔债，给教会整个换一副新底子。这话本不该我自己说；可在我待过的别处，我的讲道在镇上也算得上——算得上一景。我一向习惯把许多会外的人吸到我们的礼拜来；您知道，从钱眼上看，这有天大的好处。可不知怎么，在这里失灵了。我疑心如今平均的会众，比我四月来时大不出一根头发。捐项我是知道没有大。」
「不，」律师缓缓地说；「这一路，您在奥克塔维乌斯永远做不出名堂。当然，您若在这儿扎下、苦干它五年六年，也许——」
「上天不容！」韦尔先生呻吟。
「正是，」那一位接口。「关键在于：这里的美以美会自成一小撮。我不敢说他们彼此多么喜欢；可我知道，他们在圈外人的眼里谈不上得人心。换一个新牧师到长老会去，甚至浸礼会去——兴许还有机会闹出动静。美以美会——不成！只要城里还有别的带屋顶的地方可去，会外的人就不会踏进这里的美以美会。恶名一沾身，您知道的。这里的美以美会就沾了恶名；您就是讲得赛过亨利·沃德·比彻本人，也改不了它，也招不来人听。」
「我明白您的意思，」塞隆应道。「奥克塔维乌斯不爱我们、不指望我们供给一点思想的激刺，我自己也并不特别惊奇。我自己呢，从那个讲坛上下来，多半时候也像一条湿抹布——浑身瘫软，心灰意懒。不过——恕我提起——*您*是会外的人，可*您*来。」
律师显然不介意。他坦然作答。「哦，是，我习惯了。

===Original===

I began going when I first came here, and﻿—and so it grew to be natural for me to go. Then, of course, being the only lawyer you have, a considerable amount of my business is mixed up in one way or another with your membership; you see those are really the things which settle a man in a rut, and keep him there.”
“I suppose your people were Methodists,” said Theron, to fill in the pause, “and that is how you originally started with us.”
Levi Gorringe shook his head. He leaned back, half closed his eyes, put his fingertips together, and almost smiled as if something in retrospect pleased and moved him.
“No,” he said; “I went to the church first to see a girl who used to go there. It was long before your time. All her family moved away years ago. You wouldn’t know any of them. I was younger then, and I didn’t know as much as I do now. I worshipped the very ground that girl walked on, and like a fool I never gave her so much as a hint of it. Looking back now, I can see that I might have had her if I’d asked her. But I went instead and sat around and looked at her at church and Sunday-school and prayer-meetings Thursday nights, and class-meetings after the sermon. She was devoted to religion and church work; and, thinking it would please her, I joined the church on probation. Men can fool themselves easier than they can other people. I actually believed at the time that I had experienced religion. I felt myself full of all sorts of awakenings of the soul and so forth. But it was really that girl. You see I’m telling you the thing just as it was. I was very happy. I think it was the happiest time of my life. I remember there was a love-feast while I was on probation; and I sat down in front, right beside her, and we ate the little square chunks of bread and drank the water together, and I held one corner of her hymnbook when we stood up and sang. That was the nearest I ever got to her, or to full membership in the church. That very next week, I think it was, we learned that she had got engaged to the minister’s son﻿—a young man who had just become a minister himself. They got married, and went away﻿—and I﻿—somehow I never took up my membership when the six months’ probation was over. That’s how it was.”
“It is very interesting,” remarked Theron, softly, after a little silence﻿—“and very full of human nature.”

===Chinese===

我初来时就开始去，后来——去惯了，成了自然。再说，我又是你们唯一的律师，我的事务有相当一部分同你们的教友这样那样地缠着；您瞧，正是这些事情，把一个人安进车辙，又把他钉在那儿。」
「我猜您家里原是美以美会的人，」塞隆说，填补这阵停顿，「您当初就是这样从我们起的头。」
利维·戈林奇摇头。他往后靠，半阖上眼，指尖对指尖，几乎笑了——仿佛回望中的什么东西又叫他喜欢、又叫他动情。
「不，」他说；「我起初上那教堂，是为了看一个常去的姑娘。那是远在您来之前。她全家多年前就迁走了。他们一个您也不会认得。那时我年轻，懂得的没有如今多。她脚下踩的土我都拜。可我像个傻子，连一星半点也没让她知道。如今回头看，我明白，只要我开过口，她本可以是我的。可我没开口，只是跑去坐在教堂里、主日学校里、星期四晚上的祷告会上、讲道后的班会上，望着她。她一心虔奉宗教和教会的事工；我满以为入教会讨她欢心，就试道入会。人骗自己，总比骗别人容易。我当时当真相信自己经历了宗教。我觉得自己满心是灵魂的种种觉醒，等等。可其实那是那个姑娘。您瞧，我把事情原原本本都告诉您了。我很快活。我想那是我一生最快活的日子。记得我试道期里赶上一场爱筵；我坐在前排，就挨着她；我们一起吃那一小块一小块的方面包，一起喝水；起身唱诗的时候，我捾着她的赞美诗集的一角。我离她最近，就到这一步；离教会的正式会员资格，也就到这一步。我记得就是紧接着的那个星期，我们听说她同牧师的儿子订了婚——一个自己也刚做牧师的年轻人。他们结了婚，走了——我呢——六个月试道期满，也不知怎么没有续办入会。就是这么回事。」
「很有意思，」沉默片刻后塞隆轻声说——「很见人性。」

===Original===

“Well, now you see,” said the lawyer, “what I mean when I say that there hasn’t been another minister here since, that I should have felt like telling this story to. They wouldn’t have understood it at all. They would have thought it was blasphemy for me to say straight out that what I took for experiencing religion was really a girl. But you are different. I felt that at once, the first time I saw you. In a pulpit or out of it, what I like in a human being is that he *should* be human.”
“It pleases me beyond measure that you should like me, then,” returned the young minister, with frank gratification shining on his face. “The world is made all the sweeter and more lovable by these﻿—these elements of romance. I am not one of those who would wish to see them banished or frowned upon. I don’t mind admitting to you that there is a good deal in Methodism﻿—I mean the strict practice of its letter which you find here in Octavius﻿—that is personally distasteful to me. I read the other day of an English bishop who said boldly, publicly, that no modern nation could practise the principles laid down in the Sermon on the Mount and survive for twenty-four hours.”
“Ha, ha! That’s good!” laughed the lawyer.
“I felt that it was good, too,” pursued Theron. “I am getting to see a great many things differently, here in Octavius. Our Methodist Discipline is like the Beatitudes﻿—very helpful and beautiful, if treated as spiritual suggestion, but more or less of a stumbling-block if insisted upon literally. I declare!” he added, sitting up in his chair, “I never talked like this to a living soul before in all my life. Your confidences were contagious.”
The Rev. Mr. Ware rose as he spoke, and took up his hat.
“Must you be going?” asked the lawyer, also rising. “Well, I’m glad I haven’t shocked you. Come in oftener when you are passing. And if you see anything I can help you in, always tell me.”
The two men shook hands, with an emphatic and lingering clasp.
“*I* am glad,” said Theron, “that you didn’t stop coming to church just because you lost the girl.”
Levi Gorringe answered the minister’s pleasantry with a smile which curled his mustache upward, and expanded in little wrinkles at the ends of his eyes. “No,” he said jestingly. “I’m death on collecting debts; and I reckon that the church still owes me a girl. I’ll have one yet.”

===Chinese===

「那么您现在明白，」律师说，「我为什么说：此后来的历任牧师里，再没有一个让我想把这故事讲给他听的。他们根本不会懂。我直截了当说出『我当作宗教经历的那个东西，其实是一个姑娘』，他们准以为是亵渎。可您不同。我头一回见您，立刻就觉出来了。在讲坛上也罢，出了讲坛也罢，一个人身上我喜欢的，就是他还*是*一个人。」
「那么您喜欢我，这真叫我喜出望外，」年轻的牧师答，脸上放着坦然的得意。「世界正是被这些——这些罗曼司的成分，添得又甜又可爱。我不是那类巴不得把它们赶尽杀绝、见着就皱眉的人。我不妨向您承认：美以美会——我是说您在奥克塔维乌斯见的这严苛的字面奉行——有许多处，我个人很不喜欢。前些天我读到，英国有位主教公然大胆地说：现代国家里没有一个，能把登山宝训立下的原则实行一天二十四小时，还能存活。」
「哈，哈！说得好！」律师笑起来。
「我也觉得说得好，」塞隆接着说。「在奥克塔维乌斯，我渐渐把许多事情看开了。我们的美以美《规程》就像那八福——当灵性的指引用，大有裨益，非常美好；硬按字面坚持，就多少成了绊脚石。真的！」他在椅子里坐直了，添一句，「我生平从没对任何人这样讲过话。您的知心话会传染。」
韦尔牧师说着站起身，拿起了帽子。
「这就得走了？」律师也起身问。「好，总算没有吓着您。路过就常来坐。要是看见什么我帮得上忙的地方，随时告诉我。」
两人握手，握得又重又久。
「*我*高兴的是，」塞隆说，「您没有因为丢了那位姑娘，就不上教堂了。」
利维·戈林奇用一记微笑回答牧师的玩笑——髭须朝上卷，眼梢绽开细细的纹。「不，」他打趣道。「我讨账是出了名地狠；我算教会还欠我一个姑娘呢。早晚我要讨到手一个。」

===Original===

So, with merriment the echoes of which pleasantly accompanied Theron down the stairway, the two men parted.

===Chinese===

两人就这样分手了；那阵欢笑的余音，一路愉快地送着塞隆走下楼梯。

### XIII / 十三
===Original===

Though time lagged in passing with a slowness which seemed born of studied insolence, there did arrive at last a day which had something definitive about it to Theron’s disturbed and restless mind. It was a Thursday, and the prayer-meeting to be held that evening would be the last before the Quarterly Conference, now only four days off.
For some reason, the young minister found himself dwelling upon this fact, and investing it with importance. But yesterday the Quarterly Conference had seemed a long way ahead. Today brought it alarmingly close to hand. He had not heretofore regarded the weekly assemblage for prayer and song as a thing calling for preparation, or for any preliminary thought. Now on this Thursday morning he went to his desk after breakfast, which was a sign that he wanted the room to himself, quite as if he had the task of a weighty sermon before him. He sat at the desk all the forenoon, doing no writing, it is true, but remembering every once in a while, when his mind turned aside from the book in his hands, that there was that prayer-meeting in the evening.
Sometimes he reached the point of vaguely wondering why this strictly commonplace affair should be forcing itself thus upon his attention. Then, with a kind of mental shiver at the recollection that this was Thursday, and that the great struggle came on Monday, he would go back to his book.
There were a half-dozen volumes on the open desk before him. He had taken them out from beneath a pile of old *Sunday-School Advocate*s and church magazines, where they had lain hidden from Alice’s view most of the week. If there had been a locked drawer in the house, he would have used it instead to hold these books, which had come to him in a neat parcel, which also contained an amiable note from Dr. Ledsmar, recalling a pleasant evening in May, and expressing the hope that the accompanying works would be of some service. Theron had glanced at the backs of the uppermost two, and discovered that their author was Renan. Then he had hastily put the lot in the best place he could think of to escape his wife’s observation.
He realized now that there had been no need for this secrecy. Of the other four books, by Sayce, Budge, Smith, and Lenormant, three indeed revealed themselves to be published under religious auspices. As for Renan, he might have known that the name would be meaningless to Alice.

===Chinese===

时间过得那样慢，慢得像出于存心的无礼，可是终于到了这么一天，在塞隆那纷乱不宁的心里，它自有一锤定音的分量。这是个星期四，晚间这场祷告会，是季度议会前最后的一场——议会只剩四天了。
不知怎么，年轻的牧师发现自己老惦着这个事实，把它抬得很重。就在昨天，季度议会还显得远在天边；今天它已惊人地逼近眼前。他一向不曾把每周的祷告唱诗聚会看作需要预备、需要预先思量的事。可这个星期四的早晨，早饭后他便到了书桌前——这是他要独用房间的标志——俨然面前摆着一篇重大讲道的差事。整个上午他坐在桌前，固然一字未写，可是每当心思从手中那本书上溜开，便记起晚间那场祷告会。
有时他隐约纳闷，为什么这样一件彻头彻尾的寻常事，硬要这样挤进他的注意。随后，想起今天是星期四、大战在星期一，他心里一颤，又回到书里。
敞开的书桌前摆着六本书。他把它们从一摸旧《主日学倡导者》和教会杂志底下取了出来——一个星期的大半时间，它们一直藏在那里，避开艾丽斯的眼睛。家里若有一只上锁的抽屉，他早就用它来装这些书了。它们来时是齐整的一包，随包还附着莱兹马医生一封友好的短简，追忆五月里那个愉快的夜晚，并希望随函奉上的著作能有点用处。塞隆扫了一眼最上面两本的书脊，发现作者是勒南。于是他勿勿把这一摸塞进他所能想到的最好的地方，躲开妻子的耳目。
他此刻明白，这份遮掩原无必要。另外四本，塞斯、巴奇、史密斯和勒诺芒著的四本里，倒有三本一看便知是在宗教机构的名义下出版的。至于勒南，他本该想到，这名字对艾丽斯毫无意义。

The feeling that he himself was not much wiser in this matter than his wife may have led him to pass over the learned textbooks on Chaldean antiquity, and even the volume of Renan which appeared to be devoted to Oriental inscriptions, and take up his other book, entitled in the translation, *Recollections of My Youth*. This he rather glanced through, at the outset, following with a certain inattention the introductory sketches and essays, which dealt with an unfamiliar, and, to his notion, somewhat preposterous Breton racial type. Then, little by little, it dawned upon him that there was a connected story in all this; and suddenly he came upon it, out in the open, as it were. It was the story of how a deeply devout young man, trained from his earliest boyhood for the sacred office, and desiring passionately nothing but to be worthy of it, came to a point where, at infinite cost of pain to himself and of anguish to those dearest to him, he had to declare that he could no longer believe at all in revealed religion.
Theron Ware read this all with an excited interest which no book had ever stirred in him before. Much of it he read over and over again, to make sure that he penetrated everywhere the husk of French habits of thought and Catholic methods in which the kernel was wrapped. He broke off midway in this part of the book to go out to the kitchen to dinner, and began the meal in silence. To Alice’s questions he replied briefly that he was preparing himself for the evening’s prayer-meeting. She lifted her brows in such frank surprise at this that he made a further and somewhat rambling explanation about having again taken up the work on his book﻿—the book about Abraham.
“I thought you said you’d given that up altogether,” she remarked.
“Well,” he said, “I *was* discouraged about it for a while. But a man never does anything big without getting discouraged over and over again while he’s doing it. I don’t say now that I shall write precisely *that* book﻿—I’m merely reading scientific works about the period, just now﻿—but if not that, I shall write some other book. Else how will you get that piano?” he added, with an attempt at a smile.
“I thought you had given that up, too!” she replied ruefully. Then before he could speak, she went on: “Never mind the piano; that can wait. What I’ve got on my mind just now isn’t piano; it’s potatoes.

自觉在这件事上并不比妻子高明多少的心情，也许正是他放过那几部迦勒底古学的学术教本、连那部论东方铭文的勒南也放过的缘故；他拿起的是另一本，书名译出来是《青年回忆录》。起初他不过翻翻，心不在焉地跟着那些导论性的速写和文章走——它们写的是一个他不熟悉的、而且在他看来有些荒唐的布列塔尼种族类型。渐渐地，他醒悟这一切里面有一个连贯的故事；随后他猛地撞上了它——公开地、赤裸裸地摆在那里。这是一个青年如何矢志虔诚、自幼受训担任圣职、一心一念只想配得上它，却到了这样一个地步：以自己无穷的痛苦、以至亲之人无尽的悲怆为代价，不得不宣布自己再也不能相信任何天启宗教。
塞隆·韦尔读着这一切，怀着哪一本书都未曾在他身上激起过的兴奋的兴味。许多段落他一遍又一遍地读，务求处处穿透那层法式思维习惯和天主教方式的外壳，咬到里面的核。他读到这一部分的中途，撂下书去厨房吃饭，一餐起头默默无言。对艾丽斯的问话，他简短地答道，他在为晚间的祷告会预备自己。她扬起眉毛，那份坦率的惊讶，逼得他又作了一番颇为散漫的解释，说他重新拾起了那本书的工作——那本关于亚伯拉罕的书。
“我记得你说过，已经整个儿放弃了。”她说。
“嗯，”他说，“我*是*灰心过一阵子。可一个人不做大事则已，做大事，总得一遍又一遍地灰心。我现在不是说我准写*那本*书——眼下我不过读些关于那个时代的科学著作——可不写那本，也会写别的书。不然，你的钢琴从哪儿来？”他添了一句，赔了个笑。
“我还当那个你也放弃了！”她懊丧地答道。不等他开口，她接着说：“钢琴不用管；那个等得起。我这会儿压在心上的不是钢琴，是土豆。

===Original===

Do you know, I saw some the other day at Rasbach’s, splendid potatoes﻿—these are some of them﻿—and fifteen cents a bushel cheaper than those dried-up old things Brother Barnum keeps, and so I bought two bushels. And Sister Barnum met me on the street this morning, and threw it in my face that the Discipline commands us to trade with each other. Is there any such command?”
“Yes,” said the husband. “It’s Section 33. Don’t you remember? I looked it up in Tyre. We are to ‘evidence our desire of salvation by doing good, especially to them that are of the household of faith, or groaning so to be; by employing them preferably to others; buying one of another; helping each other in business’﻿—and so on. Yes, it’s all there.”
“Well, I told her I didn’t believe it was,” put in Alice, “and I said that even if it was, there ought to be another section about selling potatoes to their minister for more than they’re worth﻿—potatoes that turn all green when you boil them, too. I believe I’ll read up that old Discipline myself, and see if it hasn’t got some things that I can talk back with.”
“The very section before that, Number 32, enjoins members against ‘uncharitable or unprofitable conversation﻿—particularly speaking evil of magistrates or ministers.’ You’d have ’em there, I think.” Theron had begun cheerfully enough, but the careworn, preoccupied look returned now to his face. “I’m sorry if we’ve fallen out with the Barnums,” he said. “His brother-in-law, Davis, the Sunday-school superintendent, is a member of the Quarterly Conference, you know, and I’ve been hoping that he was on my side. I’ve been taking a good deal of pains to make up to him.”
He ended with a sigh, the pathos of which impressed Alice. “If you think it will do any good,” she volunteered, “I’ll go and call on the Davises this very afternoon. I’m sure to find her at home﻿—she’s tied hand and foot with that brood of hers﻿—and you’d better give me some of that candy for them.”
Theron nodded his approval and thanks, and relapsed into silence. When the meal was over, he brought out the confectionery to his wife, and without a word went back to that remarkable book.
When Alice returned toward the close of day, to prepare the simple tea which was always laid a half-hour earlier on Thursdays and Sundays, she found her husband where she had left him, still busy with those new scientific works.

“你知道吗，前几天我在拉斯巴赫铺子里看见一些，好极了的土豆——喏，就是这些——比巴纳姆弟兄那些干瘪老货一蒲式耳便宜一角五分，我就买了两蒲式耳。今儿早上巴纳姆姊妹在街上遇见我，当面摔给我一句：教规命令咱们彼此做买卖。有这么条命令么？”
“有，”丈夫说，“是第三十三条。不记得了么？在泰尔那会儿我查过。咱们应当‘以行善显明得救的愿望，尤其施于信家之人，或渴慕信家之人；优先雇用他们；彼此购销；在生意上互相扶助’——等等。是啊，全在那儿。”
“哼，我跟她说我不信有这条，”艾丽斯接口道，“我还说，就算有，也该另加一条，管管把土豆卖给他们牧师还高出市价的事——那种一煮就发绿的土豆。我看我要亲自把那老教规啃一遍，看看它有没有几样我能拿去回嘴的。”
“前一条，第三十二条，正告诫会友不得作‘无爱心的或无益的谈话——尤其不可毁谤官长或牧师’。你可叫他们拿住了，我看。”塞隆起头还够轻快，此刻那忧心忡忡、神不守舍的神色又回到了脸上。“同巴纳姆家闹翻，我很难过，”他说，“他内弟戴维斯，主日学校的校长，是季度议会的成员，你知道；我一向指望他站在我这边。我在他身上下过一番结交的功夫。”
他叹了口气收尾，那叹息的哀意打动了艾丽斯。“你要是觉得有用，”她自告奋勇，“今天下午我就去拜访戴维斯家。她准在家——她叫那一窝孩子拴得死死的——你最好给我拿点那种糖果，带给孩子们。”
塞隆点头致谢，又归于沉默。饭毕，他把糖果交出来给妻子，一言不发地回到那本了不起的书跟前。
傍晚艾丽斯回来预备简单的茶饭——星期四和礼拜天，茶饭总提前半小时摆——她见丈夫还在她离开时的老地方，还忙着那些新的科学著作。

She recounted to him some incidents of her call upon Mrs. Davis, as she took off her hat and put on the big kitchen apron﻿—how pleased Mrs. Davis seemed to be; how her affection for her sister-in-law, the grocer’s wife, disclosed itself to be not even skin-deep; how the children leaped upon the candy as if they had never seen any before; and how, in her belief, Mr. Davis would be heart and soul on Theron’s side at the Conference.
To her surprise, the young minister seemed not at all interested. He hardly looked at her during her narrative, but reclined in the easy-chair with his head thrown back, and an abstracted gaze wandering aimlessly about the ceiling. When she avowed her faith in the Sunday-school superintendent’s loyal partisanship, which she did with a pardonable pride in having helped to make it secure, her husband even closed his eyes, and moved his head with a gesture which plainly bespoke indifference.
“I expected you’d be tickled to death,” she remarked, with evident disappointment.
“I’ve a bad headache,” he explained, after a minute’s pause.
“No wonder!” Alice rejoined, sympathetically enough, but with a note of reproof as well. “What can you expect, staying cooped up in here all day long, poring over those books? People are all the while remarking that you study too much. I tell them, of course, that you’re a great hand for reading, and always were; but I think myself it would be better if you got out more, and took more exercise, and saw people. You know lots and slathers more than *they* do now, or ever will, if you never opened another book.”
Theron regarded her with an expression which she had never seen on his face before. “You don’t realize what you are saying,” he replied slowly. He sighed as he added, with increased gravity, “I am the most ignorant man alive!”
Alice began a little laugh of wifely incredulity, and then let it die away as she recognized that he was really troubled and sad in his mind. She bent over to kiss him lightly on the brow, and tiptoed her way out into the kitchen.
“I believe I will let you make my excuses at the prayer-meeting this evening,” he said all at once, as the supper came to an end. He had eaten next to nothing during the meal, and had sat in a sort of brown-study from which Alice kindly forbore to arouse him. “I don’t know﻿—I hardly feel equal to it.

出乎她意料，年轻的牧师似乎毫无兴趣。她叙述的当儿，他几乎不看她，只仰在大安乐椅里，出神的目光在天花板上漫无目的地游。待她表白对那位主日学校长的忠心党谊的信心——表白时不无一点有可原宥的得意，因为那忠心有她一份巩固之功——丈夫竟索性阖上眼，摆一摆头，那姿态明明白白说的是漠然。
“我还当你会乐坏呢。”她说，明显地失望了。
“我头疼得厉害。”过了一分钟他才解释。
“难怪！”艾丽斯应道，同情固然够同情，却也带着一点责备的调子。“成天闷在这儿，埋头啻这些书，还能指望怎样？人家老是说你用功太过。我当然告诉他们，你天生是个书虫，一向如此；可我自己也觉得，你多出去走走、多活动活动、多见见人，要好些。你就是再不翻开一本书，你懂的也比*他们*多得多，比他们一辈子懂的都多。”

艾丽斯起了一个妻子式的不信的浅笑，随即认出他是真烦真愁，那笑便半途消散了。她俯身在他额上轻轻一吻，踮着脚尖出了厨房。
“我看今晚祷告会，就请你替我告个假吧。”晚饭将尽时他忽然说。整顿饭他几乎没吃什么，一直坐在一种痴想里，艾丽斯好心没有惊动他。“我不知道——我怕撑不下来。

===Original===

They won’t take it amiss﻿—for once﻿—if you explain to them that I﻿—I am not at all well.”
“Oh, I do hope you’re not coming down with anything!” Alice had risen too, and was gazing at him with a solicitude the tenderness of which at once comforted, and in some obscure way jarred on his nerves. “Is there anything I can do﻿—or shall I go for a doctor? We’ve got mustard in the house, and senna﻿—I think there’s some senna left﻿—and Jamaica ginger.”
Theron shook his head wearily at her. “Oh, no﻿—no!” he expostulated. “It isn’t anything that needs drugs, or doctors either. It’s just mental worry and fatigue, that’s all. An evening’s quiet rest in the big chair, and early to bed﻿—that will fix me up all right.”
“But you’ll read; and that will make your head worse,” said Alice.
“No, I won’t read any more,” he promised her, walking slowly into the sitting-room, and settling himself in the big chair, the while she brought out a pillow from the adjoining best bedroom, and adjusted it behind his head. “That’s nice! I’ll just lie quiet here, and perhaps doze a little till you come back. I feel in the mood for the rest; it will do me all sorts of good.”
He closed his eyes; and Alice, regarding his upturned face anxiously, decided that already it looked more at peace than awhile ago.
“Well, I hope you’ll be better when I get back,” she said, as she began preparations for the evening service. These consisted in combing stiffly back the strands of light-brown hair which, during the day, had exuberantly loosened themselves over her temples into something almost like curls; in fastening down upon this rebellious hair a plain brown-straw bonnet, guiltless of all ornament save a binding ribbon of dull umber hue; and in putting on a thin dark-gray shawl and a pair of equally subdued lisle-thread gloves. Thus attired, she made a mischievous little grimace of dislike at her puritanical image in the looking-glass over the mantel, and then turned to announce her departure.
“Well, I’m off,” she said. Theron opened his eyes to take in this figure of his wife dressed for prayer-meeting, and then closed them again abruptly. “All right,” he murmured, and then he heard the door shut behind her.
Although he had been alone all day, there seemed to be quite a unique value and quality in this present solitude.

“要是你向他们说明我——我身体很不舒服，就这一回，他们不会见怪的。”
“哦，你可千万别是染上什么了！”艾丽斯也站了起来，凝望着他，那份关切里的温存，一时安慰了他，又不知怎么擦痛了他的神经。“有什么我能做的么——要不要请大夫？家里有芥末，有番泻叶——我记得还剩一点——还有牙买加姜。”
塞隆疲惫地朝她摇头。“哦，不——不！”他分辩道，“这病不用药，也不用大夫。不过是精神的烦劳，如此而已。晚上在大安乐椅里静静歇一歇，早点睡——就会把我养好的。”
“可你还要看书；那头就更疼了。”艾丽斯说。
“不，我再也不看了。”他向她保证，慢慢践进起居室，在大安乐椅里安顿下来；她则从隔壁那间上房拿出一只枕头，替他垫在脑后。“好极了！我就这么静静躺着，兴许迷糊到你回来。我正想歇歇；这于我有百利。”
他阖上眼；艾丽斯端详着他仰起的脸，断定它已经比方才安详了些。
“好，望我回来时你好些。”她说着，开始做出门的预备。这预备包括：把白天在鬓边松散得几乎成卷的浅棕色发绺，板板地朝后梳拢；在这不服帖的头发上扣一顶素净的棕色麦秆帽——除一条暗褐色系带外别无装饰；再披上一条薄薄的深灰披肩，戴上一双同样素淡的棉线手套。装束停当，她朝壁炉架上镜子里自己那副清教徒模样，做个嫌恶的淘气鬼脸，然后转身宣布动身。
“那么，我走啦。”她说。塞隆睁开眼，把这身祷告会装束的妻子收入一望，随即又蓦地阖上。“好。”他咕哝道；随后他听见门在她身后关上了。
他虽然独处了一整天，此刻这份清静，却自有一种独特的价值与质地。

===Original===

He stretched out his legs on the opposite chair, and looked lazily about him, with the feeling that at last he had secured some leisure, and could think undisturbed to his heart’s content. There were nearly two hours of unbroken quiet before him; and the mere fact of his having stepped aside from the routine of his duty to procure it; marked it in his thoughts as a special occasion, which ought in the nature of things to yield more than the ordinary harvest of mental profit.
Theron’s musings were broken in upon from time to time by rumbling outbursts of hymn-singing from the church next door. Surely, he said to himself, there could be no other congregation in the Conference, or in all Methodism, which sang so badly as these Octavians did. The noise, as it came to him now and again, divided itself familiarly into a main strain of hard, high, sharp, and tinny female voices, with three or four concurrent and clashing branch strains of part-singing by men who did not know how. How well he already knew these voices! Through two wooden walls he could detect the conceited and pushing note of Brother Lovejoy, who tried always to drown the rest out, and the lifeless, unmeasured weight of shrill clamor which Sister Barnum hurled into every chorus, half closing her eyes and sticking out her chin as she did so. They drawled their hymns too, these people, till Theron thought he understood that injunction in the Discipline against singing too slowly. It had puzzled him heretofore; now he felt that it must have been meant in prophecy for Octavius.
It was impossible not to recall in contrast that other church music he had heard, a month before, and the whole atmosphere of that other pastoral sitting room, from which he had listened to it. The startled and crowded impressions of that strange evening had been lying hidden in his mind all this while, driven into a corner by the pressure of more ordinary, everyday matters. They came forth now, and passed across his brain﻿—no longer confusing and distorted, but in orderly and intelligible sequence. Their earlier effect had been one of frightened fascination. Now he looked them over calmly as they lifted themselves, one by one, and found himself not shrinking at all, or evading anything, but dwelling upon each in turn as a natural and welcome part of the most important experience of his life.

===Chinese===
